 Rules & Regulations

 Chipalooza Challenge #4 (Chip Foundry Sky130) · November 9, 2026–May 19, 2027

 The official name of the challenge is “Chipalooza Challenge #4 (Chip Foundry Sky130)”. This challenge may be referred to as “Chipalooza Challenge #4”. It may also be referred to informally as “Chip Foundry (2)” or “Sky130 (2)”. All designs created for this challenge must conform to all requirements of the Sky130 process (i.e., DRC, voltage limits, etc.).
 This challenge is designated as the second SkyWater challenge because it is considered to be the follow-up of the original Efabless Chipalooza Challenge for SkyWater Sky130 in 2024. Efabless is gone, but Chip Foundry continues the Sky130 shuttle runs, and Open Circuit Design continues the challenges.

 The official start date for the Chipalooza Challenge #4 is Monday, November 9, 2026.

 The challenge is 27 weeks long, counting from the start date and ending on the tapeout date. The aim is to keep a schedule similar to previous challenges, ending with final design reviews at the end of February.

 Designers may work as individuals or as a team. It is preferable to keep teams to three people or fewer, but not a requirement.

 Proposals are due two weeks from the beginning of the challenge, which is November 23, 2026.

# claude-cloud-config

A prebuilt open-source analog EDA toolchain, pinned to the **IIC-OSIC-TOOLS 2026.10** versions, and a setup
setup scripts for **Claude Code cloud sessions**. Made for Chipalooza 2026 designs in IHP **sg13cmos5l**, so that
cloud results come from the same tool versions as the IIC-OSIC-TOOLS container. The same tools are also set up with
**sky130A** and **gf180mcuD** (one PDK per cloud environment; see "Which script when" for what has been tested).

Why prebuilt: a Claude Code cloud environment is cached only if its setup script finishes in about five minutes.
Compiling ngspice alone takes almost that long, so a compiling setup script is never cached and gets cut off.
This setup script only downloads and unpacks.

## Use

Paste the setup script of your PDK (table below) into the cloud environment's **Setup script** (claude.ai/code: the
caret after the session title, then **Edit cloud environment**, then **Setup script**; save with **Save changes**).
Network access: the default package managers plus `www.klayout.org`; for sky130A and gf180mcuD also the hosts that
ciel downloads from (`fossi-
… [4909 more characters cut]

5	# Slot 7: custom S2D / D2S pad pair with a bandgap-referenced replica bias (as taped out, November 2026)
6	
7	Designer: Christoph Maier. Slot 7 turns a single-ended pad signal into a differential pair and back: two custom analog pads, a bandgap, a
8	bias distribution and replica-bias block that give the pads their reference currents and common-mode / reference voltages, and a small
9	3.3 V control block with fail-safe enables. Sections 2 and 3 are the electrical documentation and the state of readiness of each block (the glue logic, which is as important as the circuits, has its own page: [glue_logic.md](glue_logic.md));
10	the pins, layout hierarchy and verification follow; the tughra art is an aside at the end. Running log of how this page was assembled:
11	[log.md](log.md). # Slot 7 glue logic: enables, mode decode, switch matrix, bias configuration, `dig_out` pull-downs

The glue is everything in `slot_7` that is not a signal or reference circuit: it makes the slot's enables, decodes six `dig_in` bits into modes, steers the
differential signal between the two pads and the harness buses, switches the replica-bias outputs onto the pads, configures the bias distribution, and keeps the unused
`dig_out` low. It was written in the replica-bias / slot hook-up session on 2026-11-08 on the day before the tapeout commit (2026-11-09), from the designer's mode specification, in the netlist
[`netlist/slot_7.spice`](https://github.com/tatzelbrumm/sg13cmos5l_cm_slot7/blob/158afc5afab0056bd6d216a4db3833e34a364949/netlist/slot_7.spice)
(subcircuits `s7_ctrl`, `s7_doutpd`, `slot_7`); every statement below about its logic is derived from that netlist, from which the CDL used for the layout check was derived
(KLayout LVS deep match, 497 devices). Back to [README.md](README.md).

## 1. Cells

| cell | what | where it comes from |
|---|---|---|
| `ls_up_fs` (core `efs_ls_core`) ×7 | fail-safe level shifter, one per input bit: 1.2 V harness level → 3.3 V (`out`, `out_b`) | `macros/en_ctrl_3v3` v2 |
| `nand2_hv` ×13, `inv_hv` ×12, `tg_hv` ×10 | 3.3 V static CMOS gates and transmission gates, all 1 µm / 0.45 µm sg13_hv (NOR stacks wider) | `macros/hv_logic`, layouts as `bdp_*` cel
… [4445 more characters cut]
```

<!--

# Proposal, Chipalooza Challenge #4 (Chip Foundry Sky130): custom single-to-differential and differential-to-single analog pads with selectable reference circuits

Status: proposal of 2026-11-23. Everything stated for sky130 is a **target** (a proposal) unless it is explicitly labelled
*sky130 pre-layout schematic simulation (feasibility, not sign-off)*. Observed values quoted from the predecessor design are
**IHP sg13cmos5l simulation results**, not sky130 results; their source files and commits are listed in
[SOURCES.md](SOURCES.md). "TBD" marks what is not known yet, with what determines it.

Contents: 1 Type of IP block · 2 I/O list including test ports · 3 Functional description · 4 Target specification ·
5 Test plan outline · 6 Port plan · 7 Risks · 8 Schedule · 9 License · 10 AI assistance

---

## 1. Type of IP block

**A proof of concept of custom analog pads**, ported from a design taped out on IHP sg13cmos5l (Chipalooza Challenge #2, slot 7,
November 2026), consisting of:

- **S2D pad**: a buffered single-ended-in, differential-out analog input pad. Pad voltage in, differential pair out:
  `outp − outn = 2 (pad − vref)`, `(outp + outn)/2 = vcm`.
- **D2S pad**: a buffered differential-in, single-ended-out class-AB analog output pad that drives the bond pad through
  output devices that are part of the pad's own clamp structure: `pad − vref = (vinp − vinn)/2`.

Together they carry a single-ended off-chip signal into the core as a differential signal and back out, with gain set by
device matching rather than by resistor ratios. For an SoC this is the analog front and back end that a fully differential
core (filter, ADC driver, DAC output) needs to talk to the single-ended outside world, packed into the pad frame instead of
the core area, with fail-safe enables and low leakage when unpowered.

**The pads are the part under review for this proposal.** The supporting circuits are a **fallback for testing** and are
also reusable on their own: an on-slot Oguey–Aebischer MOS-only current reference, an on-slot Banba current-mode bandgap
current reference, a switchable bias distribution, replica generators for `vcm` and `vref`, and 3.3 V mode / enable glue
with fail-safe level shifters. Their specifications are **tentative**, are meant to be better than those of the IHP
predecessor, and are to be reviewed at the final design review. Each supporting circuit can be bypassed by a harness
resource (section 3.4), so the pads can be measured even if an on-slot reference misbehaves.

The block is usable as a unit (pads + references + glue) and as separately reusable cells (each pad alone, each reference alone).

License: Apache-2.0 or Solderpad Hardware License v2.1, pending the organizers' approval (section 9).
AI assistance: the design was created with AI assistance; using, verifying or modifying it does not require AI (section 10).

## 2. List of I/O, including test ports

Harness resources (rules-4 resource list) and what the block uses:

| resource | available | used | for |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3.3 V analog supply / ground | yes | yes | everything analog, pad buffers, references, 3.3 V glue |
| 1.8 V digital supply / ground | yes | yes (small) | only for read-back outputs and pull-downs of `dig_out`; the block works with only 3.3 V on |
| bandgap-referenced bias voltage | 1 | optional (1) | selectable source for `vcm` or `vref` (section 3.4) |
| bandgap-referenced current sources | up to 2 | optional (2) | selectable reference current: harness current 0 / harness current 1 |
| digital controls (inputs) | up to 24 | 14 + project enable | modes, key, reference and voltage-source selection (table below) |
| digital test outputs | up to 12 | 8 | read-back of the decoded mode and enables (new) |
| shared analog lines | up to 4 | 4 | differential monitor / feed (0, 1), `vcm` / `vref` monitor or drive (2, 3) |
| dedicated pins | up to 3 | 2 custom + 1 optional foundry analog pad | S2D pad, D
… [19591 more characters cut]

# License note

The license of this IP block is **either Apache-2.0 or the Solderpad Hardware License v2.1 (SHL-2.1), pending approval by
the Chipalooza organizers.**

- `LICENSE` holds the Apache-2.0 text (the license the rules prefer).
- `LICENSES/SHL-2.1.txt` holds the SHL v2.1 text (copied from the predecessor IHP slot 7 repository).
- SHL v2.1 is a wraparound license to Apache-2.0: it incorporates the Apache-2.0 terms, adapts them to hardware (rights in
  designs, mask works, databases), and is permissive; its text lets a recipient choose to treat the work as released under
  Apache-2.0.
- The rules (Chipalooza Challenge #4, rules page): "Every design must be licensed under a standard open source license,
  preferably Apache 2.0. Other similar licenses will be considered. In no case may a design use any sources which would
  violate the terms of the source's license or the Apache 2.0 license."

Until the organizers decide, files carry `SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0`; if SHL-2.1 is chosen, the identifiers become
`SHL-2.1` and `LICENSE` is replaced by the SHL text. Third-party sources used: the sky130 PDK (Apache-2.0) and the
IIC-OSIC-TOOLS tool versions (their own open-source licenses); no other third-party design sources.
EOF

<!--
SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2026 Christoph Maier
SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
-->
# AI assistance

This IP block is a **proof of concept of AI-assisted analog design**. Designer: Christoph Maier.

What was AI-assisted, in the predecessor IHP sg13cmos5l design (Chipalooza #2 slot 7) and in this repository, as the
repositories' commit histories and session records show:

- **circuits:** schematic netlists and sizing of blocks, in sessions directed by the designer (the designer specified the
  pads' function, the modes and the replica-bias concept);
- **layout scripts:** scripted layout generation (Python / KLayout) for macros and the slot assembly;
- **netlists and test benches:** SPICE netlists, CACE characterization files, test-platform benches;
- **data sheets:** generated from the committed CACE results by a script;
- **documentation:** nearly all of it, including this proposal and the sky130 simulation decks in `verification/`.

What is not AI-dependent:

- Verification uses open-source tools only (ngspice, CACE, KLayout, magic, netgen; versions recorded with each result).
- Every source needed to use, verify or modify the block is a plain file in the repository (netlists, decks, scripts,
  layouts); no AI tool, model or service is needed to run any flow or to change the design.
- The designer reviewed the results and made the design decisions; the decisions are recorded in the documents.

This statement claims no more than the repositories show: where a document or result was produced in an AI-assisted session,
it was checked by the open-source tools named in it, not by the AI.
EOF

# Equipment the test plan needs

A list of instrument *types* for [TEST_PLAN.md](TEST_PLAN.md) (no availability, no persons; the list of equipment available
is sent separately from the proposal, as the rules ask).

| instrument | used for (test-plan step) | minimum capability |
|---|---|---|
| harness test board with its SPI / control host | all steps: project select, `dig_in` routing, `analog_ena`, read-back | as provided by the harness |
| source-measure unit, 2 channels | supply currents, disabled and unpowered leakage, reference currents into 0.7 V, D2S short-circuit and output impedance | pA resolution, ±100 mA range |
| precision DC voltage source | S2D DC sweep 0 … 1 V, D2S differential drive around `vcm` (2 channels or 1 + `vcm` tracking) | 0 … 3.3 V, µV resolution |
| digital multimeters, high input impedance, 2 units | `vcm`, `vref`, S2D `outp` / `outn` on the shared lines, INL | 6½ digits, ≥ 10 GΩ input option |
| function generator | sine and step into the S2D pad | ≥ 10 MHz, low distortion (THD < −70 dB at 100 kHz) |
| oscilloscope | step response, slew, settling, enable / disable timing | ≥ 100 MHz, 4 channels |
| audio / dynamic-signal analyzer or 24-bit ADC with FFT | THD, noise spectrum | 10 Hz … 1 MHz |
| low-noise preamplifier | output noise | < 5 nV/√Hz |
| programmable supply with ripple injection (or a bias-T) | PSRR of both supplies | 3.0 … 3.6 V |
| temperature chamber or thermal forcer | temperature plan | −40 … 125 °C |
| loads | D2S load conditions | 1 kΩ, 10 / 100 / 300 pF |
EOF

# sky130_cm_ip__single2diff2single

Custom single-to-differential (S2D) and differential-to-single (D2S) analog pads with selectable reference circuits, for the
**Chipalooza Challenge #4 (Chip Foundry Sky130)**. A port of slot 7 of the IHP sg13cmos5l Chipalooza #2 chip (taped out
November 2026), proposed as a proof of concept of custom analog pads and of AI-assisted analog design.

Designer: Christoph Maier.

<!--

# Measurement test plan (sky130 block)

Outline: [PROPOSAL.md §5](PROPOSAL.md#5-outline-of-a-test-plan-for-validation-by-measurement). **Expected values** are the
target intervals of [PROPOSAL.md §4](PROPOSAL.md#4-target-specification) ("row n" below) until sky130 pre-layout and
post-layout results replace them; they are intervals to compare against, not guarantees. Register addresses of the sky130
harness are TBD (harness repository not published yet); the sequence below names harness *functions*, modelled on the
Chipalooza #2 harness procedure used for the IHP predecessor (select the project; route all digital inputs to constant 0
first; then set individual bits; enable the analog-bus switches in use). Instruments: [EQUIPMENT_NEEDED.md](EQUIPMENT_NEEDED.md).
Pin and control names: [PROPOSAL.md §2](PROPOSAL.md#2-list-of-io-including-test-ports).

Origin: adapted from the IHP test-platform procedure (`docs/slot7_testplatform/PLAN.md` §5 of the IHP design repository at
`efb80141`; supply-current subtraction, enable / disable timing, fail-safe states, bus-current measurement into an SMU at
0.7 V) and from the IHP slot 7 modes (key `dig_in[3]` = 1, `dig_in[4]` = 0; swap, mon, feed, ext). See
[SOURCES.md](SOURCES.md).

## 0. Set-up and conventions

- Every step starts from the **safe state**: project selected, all `dig_in` routed to constant 0, `enable` = 0, analog-bus
  switches open, SMUs on the buses at 0 V or open.
- Leakage baseline `I_leak` (3.3 V supply current with `enable` = 0) is taken at every temperature and subtracted from
  enabled currents (leakage grows with temperature).
- Before switching `vdda` off: bus switches open and bus sources at 0 V (on IHP a voltage on a bus drove the switch PMOS into a
  floating 3.3 V rail).
- Read-back: after every control change, `dig_out[0..7]` must show the decoded state (`en`, loop direct, loop crossed,
  S2D → bus, bus → D2S, replica connected, reference select); a mismatch stops the step.

## 1. Power-up, enable, fail-safe

| # | action | measure | expected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1.1 | `vdda` on, `enable` = 0 | `vdda` current, pad-ring current | row 34 (≤ 1 µA) |
| 1.2 | block unpowered (`vdda` off, pad ring on), `pad_s2d` and `pad_d2s` swept 0 … 3.3 V with the SMU | pad current | row 35 (±100 nA at 125 °C) |
| 1.3 | `enable` = 1, all `dig_in` = 0 | read-back = default; `vdda` and pad-ring currents | rows 32, 33 |
| 1.4 | `enable` 0 → 1 → 0, oscilloscope on `vcm` (`analog_bus2`, high-impedance probe) and on `pad_d2s` | enable time to 1 %, disable | row 31 |
| 1.5 | `enable` = 1 with `vccd` (1.8 V) off | block works (3.3 V only); read-back invalid by design | as 1.3 |

## 2. References (one at a time)

`dig_in[3]` = 1 (key), `dig_in[6..7]` = 00 / 01 / 10 / 11, `dig_in[10]` = 1 (reference current to `pad_aux`, or to
`analog_bus3` if no third pin is granted). SMU at 0.7 V sinks the current.

| # | measure | conditions | expected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2.1 | Banba output current | `vdda` 3.0 / 3.3 / 3.6 V | row 29 |
| 2.2 | Oguey–Aebischer output current | same | row 30 |
| 2.3 | harness current 0 and 1, through the same path | same | harness specification |
| 2.4 | compliance: SMU voltage 0.2 … 2.0 V | 3.3 V | change < 1 % (target; IHP test platform: < 0.02 % over 0 … 2.0 V) |
| 2.5 | enable / disable time of each core (sense resistor + oscilloscope) | 27 °C | ≤ 5 µs (row 31) |

Bus path: the shared-line switches are below 5 Ω (rules), so 5 µA drops less than 25 µV across them; the bus measurement
is valid for DC. (A sky130 pre-layout dry run of this path is in [../verification/](../verification/) when available.)

## 3. `vcm` and `vref`

Default mode (no key): `vcm` on `analog_bus2`, `vref` on `analog_bus3`; high-impedance voltmeter (≥ 10 GΩ).
For each reference select (key on, `dig_in[6..7]`): rows 27, 28. With `dig_in[8]` / `dig_in[9]` = 1: the harness voltage
appears in place of `vcm` / `vre

```bash
mkdir -p scripts && cat > scripts/render_proposal_pdf.sh <<'EOF'
#!/bin/sh
# SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2026 Christoph Maier
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
# Render docs/PROPOSAL.md to docs/PROPOSAL.pdf: pandoc (Markdown -> HTML), then headless Chromium (HTML -> PDF) via Playwright.
# Needs: pandoc, node with the playwright package and a Chromium it can launch.
set -e
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
tmp=$(mktemp -d)
cat > "$tmp/style.css" <<'CSS'
body { font-family: "DejaVu Sans", Arial, sans-serif; font-size: 9.5pt; line-height: 1.35; max-width: 100%; }
table { border-collapse: collapse; font-size: 8pt; margin: 0.5em 0; }
th, td { border: 1px solid #999; padding: 2px 4px; vertical-align: top; }
th { background: #eee; }
code { font-size: 8pt; }
h1 { font-size: 15pt; } h2 { font-size: 12pt; margin-top: 1.2em; } h3 { font-size: 10.5pt; }
CSS
pandoc docs/PROPOSAL.md -s --metadata title="Chipalooza #4 proposal" --embed-resources --css "$tmp/style.css" -o "$tmp/proposal.html"
node -e "
const { chromium } = require('playwright');
(async () => {
  const b = await chromium.launch();
  const p = await b.newPage();
  await p.goto('file://$tmp/proposal.html');
  await p.pdf({ path: 'docs/PROPOSAL.pdf', format: 'A4', margin: { top: '15mm', bottom: '15mm', left: '12mm', right: '12mm' } });
  await b.close();
})();
"
rm -rf "$tmp"
echo "docs/PROPOSAL.pdf written"
EOF
chmod +x scripts/render_proposal_pdf.sh && ./scripts/render_proposal_pdf.sh 2>&1 | tail -3; ls -la docs/PROPOSAL.pdf; python3 -c "
import re;d=open('docs/PROPOSAL.pdf','rb').read();print('pages',len(re.findall(rb'/Type\s*/Page[^s]',d)))"
```

Pushed so far:

- **New repository, `main`:**
  - `docs/PROPOSAL.md` with all five required items, plus a port plan, risk list, schedule, license and AI-assistance sections. It has no names, institutions or personal links, and there is also a 9-page `docs/PROPOSAL.pdf`.
  - The compliance matrix for the Challenge #4 rules, the test plan, the list of equipment needed, the source list, the license note, the AI-assistance note, and both license files.
- **New repository, `sudel_buecher`:** the run log, status and review checklist.

Every sky130 number in the proposal's target table is still TBD. Its observed values all come from the IHP simulation results, and I checked them against the source files.

Still running in the background:
1. sky130 simulations of the two reference circuits (the Banba bandgap and the Oguey–Aebischer core), a first device check, and a check of the measurement path through a shared bus.
2. sky130 versions of the S2D and D2S pad amplifiers.
3. The Chipalooza #2 description of slot 7 on branch `chipalooza2-documentation`: the copied document set, a test plan for the fabricated chip, sign-off reports, the AI note and the Chipalooza #2 compliance matrix. That branch is not pushed yet; I'll check the files and then push.

As each one finishes I'll check it, commit it and add its numbers to the proposal.

On the live rules page: it matches the saved copy. The Challenge #4 page names where to send the proposal: e-mail to chipalooza@opencircuitdesign.com.

Your review list is in `REVIEW_CHECKLIST.md` on `sudel_buecher`. The main decisions:
- Which controls get the new selection options: I mapped them to `dig_in[6..13]`, 14 of the 24 inputs in all.
- Default reference: I made the Banba bandgap the default, since it is the one in the taped-out IHP slot 7.
- Optional third pin: an extra foundry analog pad that brings the selected reference current out for measurement.
- Target margins: I chose the margins myself from the IHP values. The Banba and Oguey–Aebischer current targets are deliberately tighter than IHP, as you asked; check whether you agree.

## Assistant — 2026-11-23 20:41:59 UTC

On your question: I spent about 5–8 minutes of my own time on sky130 (installing the PDK and writing the two simulation briefs). The two background jobs ran in parallel for about 40 and 35 minutes. They did not hold up the proposal or the slot 7 documentation, but the IHP gap simulations could have started about 40 minutes earlier. That was my misreading of the task file's simulation section.

What changed since your message:
- **sky130 simulations stopped.** Both jobs are halted. Their files are off `main` and kept on `sudel_buecher` under `stash/sky130_sims_halted_2026-11-23/`, with a note that they are unfinished and unused.
- **sky130 wording updated.** The proposal and its supporting documents now say that sky130 simulation comes at the schematic review, built on the IHP results. The sky130 column of the target table is TBD. `PROPOSAL.pdf` is re-rendered and everything is pushed.
- **IHP gap simulations running.** Three background jobs started at 20:41 UTC (21:41 CET). They stop by 22:15 UTC at the latest. Results go to `verification/c2_gaps/` on `chipalooza2-documentation` in the slot 7 repository:
  1. **D2S pad after layout:** extract the taped-out D2S pad and rerun its characterization on the extracted netlist. Until now it had schematic results only; the S2D pad already has both.
  2. **The −40 °C mode failures:** find out whether the failures of swap, mon, feed and ext at the slow corner and −40 °C are a simulator problem or a real start-up problem of the bandgap. Also run the modes at more corners, and the bandgap alone over process, temperature and supply, with start-up ramps.
  3. **Replica bias and distribution mirror:** extract `pad_ref_rb` and rerun its characterization, and redo its mismatch Monte Carlo with the per-run data kept. Also measure how accurately `bias_dist_pd` copies the reference current.

As each job reports, I'll check its files and push them. Then I'll add the results to the slot 7 documentation and to the "basis" column of the proposal's target table.

## User (or harness) — 2026-11-23 21:01:37 UTC

how ready is the sky130 proposal? what is necessary from the ihp slot 7 characterization?

## Assistant — 2026-11-23 21:01:53 UTC

**The proposal can be submitted as it stands.** `docs/PROPOSAL.md` and `docs/PROPOSAL.pdf` (9 pages) on `main` of `sky130_cm_ip__single2diff2single` contain everything the rules ask for in a proposal:

| item | state |
|---|---|
| 1. Type of IP block | done. Pads are the core of the proposal, references are a test fallback, the gap in the Challenge #1 list is noted, plus the license and AI paragraphs. |
| 2. I/O list including test ports | done. Every pin is mapped to the resource list and to its IHP slot 7 counterpart. New controls are marked new (14 of 24 inputs, 8 test outputs, 4 buses, 2 custom pads + 1 optional third pin). The approval request for the custom pads is stated. Harness register names are TBD because the harness doesn't exist yet. |
| 3. Functional description | done: signal path, modes, enable and fail-safe, replica bias, selectable references and voltages. |
| 4. Target specification | 35 rows with min / typ / max. Every target is derived from an IHP value checked against the source files. Two things are left open: the sky130 column is TBD by your decision, and the absolute limits are "TBC from the PDK". |
| 5. Test plan outline | done, with a detailed `TEST_PLAN.md` and `EQUIPMENT_NEEDED.md`. |
| Port plan, risks, schedule, license, AI | done |

Still on your side:
- Check my margins in the target table, `REVIEW_CHECKLIST.md` item 5 on `sudel_buecher`.
- Send the list of available equipment and the CVs separately.
- E-mail the proposal to chipalooza@opencircuitdesign.com.

**What the proposal needs from the IHP slot 7 characterization:** nothing strictly. Every target row already cites IHP simulation data. The gaps make some targets weaker than they should be. In order of importance for the proposal:

1. **D2S pad after layout (job 1) matters most.** All 12 D2S rows (14–25) rest on schematic results that were simulated before its layout existed. The pads are what the proposal is about, and the rules require custom pads to be verified on their own. The S2D pad already has results on both the schematic and the extracted layout.
2. **The −40 °C mode failures (job 2) matter next.** This decides whether the bandgap start-up entry in the risk list is a simulator problem or a real circuit issue. The same job gives the bandgap its own characterization over process, temperature and supply (row 29). Right now row 29 comes from the test platform, and the Oguey–Aebischer row 30 can only ever come from there, because that core was never in the taped-out slot.
3. **Replica bias (job 3).** Rows 27–28 (`vcm` / `vref`) have schematic data only, and the existing mismatch statistics are CACE summaries with no per-run data.

Not needed for the proposal: extraction of the whole slot, mismatch runs of the control logic, the `dig_out` pull-downs. Those belong to the full IHP documentation and to the later reviews.

When the three jobs report (by 22:15 UTC at the latest), I'll add their numbers to the "basis" column of the target table. If a result contradicts a target, I'll change that target and mark the change for you.

| quantity | schematic | extracted layout |
|---|---|---|
| gain | 0.4981 … 0.5000 | 0.4983 … 0.5000 |
| output offset | 0.035 … 1.95 mV | 0.032 … 1.74 mV |
| INL | 0.12 … 7.40 mV | 0.12 … 7.50 mV |
| pad-ring supply current | 293 … 314 µA | 302 … 320 µA (+3.5 %) |
| output-stage quiescent current | 207 … 227 µA | 217 … 234 µA (+5 %) |

For these 25 runs the extracted layout matches the schematic closely, and every value stays inside the proposal's D2S targets.

## Assistant — 2026-11-23 21:09:04 UTC

The second D2S result set, the AC run, finished, and I pushed it as soon as it landed (`c744551`). It is an IHP simulation of the taped-out pad after layout extraction; all 15 runs completed.

| quantity | schematic | extracted layout |
|---|---|---|
| closed-loop bandwidth | 1.78 … 2.25 MHz | 1.57 … 2.02 MHz (up to 13 % lower) |
| supply rejection, 1 kHz | 70.7 … 72.5 dB | 70.0 … 71.9 dB |
| output impedance, 1 kHz | 0.195 … 3.85 Ω | 0.208 … 3.30 Ω |

Everything stays inside the proposal's D2S targets: at least 1 MHz bandwidth, at least 60 dB rejection, at most 10 Ω.

To get these runs through, the job had the simulator put a 1 TΩ resistor from every node to ground. That only helps the simulator converge and is far too large to change any of these values. It is recorded in the results file.

I also pushed the proposal (`7b9779c`), with `PROPOSAL.pdf` re-rendered:
- **D2S rows 14, 15, 16, 23, 24 and 25** now carry the extracted-layout values next to the schematic ones.
- **A note under the table** gives the extracted bandwidth and says that 5 of the 30 DC runs on the extracted layout have not converged in the simulator yet.
- **`SOURCES.md`** has a new entry, S9, pointing to the new results.

The latest watcher notice showed no new results: the file size was unchanged. Still running for the D2S pad: AC with load, transient, noise, drive, enable, leakage and distortion. I'll push each one as it lands.

## Assistant — 2026-11-23 21:33:01 UTC

The D2S job is done and pushed (`6f015e1`). The pad as taped out now has results after layout extraction: all 9 groups and all 127 runs, every value inside the working limits in the pad's own CACE characterization file. These are IHP simulations.

- **Extraction check:** the netlist extracted from the taped-out GDS matches the schematic (76 of 76 devices, 36 of 36 nets).
- **Main differences from the schematic:**
  - bandwidth up to 13 % lower (median 2.02 → 1.77 MHz)
  - slew rate about 13 % lower
  - turn-on time up to 94 % longer (median 0.85 → 1.35 µs)
  - quiescent current 5 % higher
  - gain, supply rejection, distortion and swing change by about 2 % or less
- **Simulator setting:** the extracted netlist only converges with the simulator's 1 TΩ resistor from every node to ground. A check at the typical corner shows it doesn't move the operating point.
- **Not done:**
  - an extraction with resistances (this one has capacitances only)
  - mismatch runs
  - five less important groups: other bias settings, the 3.0 V supply, a 1 nF load
  - ESD and latch-up simulation

The Chipalooza #2 description now carries these results (`bf64d2b`, `55a866b`). A new section 5a in `docs/chipalooza2_as_taped_out/README.md` summarizes the D2S and replica-bias results, and the readiness table has "Added 2026-11-23" notes for the D2S pad, `pad_ref_rb` and `bias_dist_pd`.

Still running: the job on the −40 °C mode failures and the bandgap, due by 22:15 UTC. Its results are partly pushed; I'll review them and add them to section 5a and the proposal when it reports.

The work is pushed and the proposal can be submitted now. The three IHP gap simulations are done and their results are in both the proposal and the Chipalooza #2 description. It is 22:00 UTC (23:00 CET), an hour before your deadline.

**Where everything is:**

| place | branch | last commit |
|---|---|---|
| `sky130_cm_ip__single2diff2single` | `main` | `400c866` |
| `sky130_cm_ip__single2diff2single` | `sudel_buecher` | `4ed3ffe` |
| `sg13cmos5l_cm_slot7` | `chipalooza2-documentation` | `ea9778f` |

- **Proposal (`main`):** `docs/PROPOSAL.md` and `PROPOSAL.pdf`, plus the test plan, equipment list, compliance matrix, sources, license note and AI note.
- **Notes (`sudel_buecher`):** `REVIEW_CHECKLIST.md`, `STATUS.md`, `RUNLOG.md`, and the stopped sky130 files under `stash/`.
- **Slot 7 as taped out (`chipalooza2-documentation`):** the Chipalooza #2 description with section 5a for today's results, the chip test plan, `signoff/`, and the simulations in `verification/c2_gaps/`.

**What today's IHP simulations showed (all IHP sg13cmos5l):**
- **D2S pad after layout extraction:** the first results for the taped-out cell beyond its schematic. All 127 runs have a result and every value stays inside the proposal's targets. The extracted netlist only converges with the simulator's 1 TΩ resistor from every node to ground; this is recorded with the results.
- **`vcm` / `vref` generator:** the extracted layout matches its schematic in DC to 0.1 mV. Mismatch σ is 5.4 mV for `vcm` and 3.8 mV for `vref` over 100 runs, now with the per-run data kept.
- **Bias distribution mirror:** it copies the reference current with +0.49 … +1.56 % systematic error.
- **The ss / −40 °C mode failures are a simulator problem, not a circuit problem.** Power-up runs settle in all 8 modes, the failure is in the pads' antenna diodes, and the bandgap alone shows no second stable state. Its current is 3.83 … 6.37 µA, set almost entirely by the resistor corner.

**Still open on the IHP side:**
- Several other mode and corner combinations still abort in the simulator before the bandgap starts (tt and sf at −40 °C, ss 125 °C in swap).
- The converted pad netlists set the antenna diodes' size with parameters the diode model doesn't use, so those diodes are simulated at default size. You should check this.
- Not run: an extraction with resistances, D2S mismatch, extraction of the whole slot, and ESD or latch-up simulation.
- No sign-off report exists for the taped-out `slot_7.gds` with the art.

**Left for you before midnight:**
- **Margins:** check the target margins (`REVIEW_CHECKLIST.md` item 5). The tightest are D2S distortion at 0.5 V (−57.7 dB against a −50 dB limit) and D2S INL (7.5 mV against 10 mV).
- **Control map:** confirm the new `dig_in` assignments and the Banba bandgap as the default reference.
- **Send:** e-mail the proposal to chipalooza@opencircuitdesign.com, and send the equipment-availability list and CVs separately.
- **Organizers decide** the custom-pad approval and the license (Apache-2.0 or SHL v2.1).

The sky130 column of the target table and the absolute limits are TBD or TBC, as you asked.

Produce DC performance graphs (input and output ranges, linearity) for the two custom pads of the taped-out IHP sg13cmos5l Chipalooza #2 slot 7, by running simulations. These go into the designer's documentation, so they must be correct, readable, labelled, and reproducible. No invented numbers: every curve and number comes from a run you make here.

The circuits:
- S2D pad `sg13cmos5l_IOPadSingle2Diff`: single-ended pad in, differential out: outp − outn = 2 (pad − vref), (outp + outn)/2 = vcm. Nominal vref ≈ 0.49 V, vcm ≈ 1.26 V (from the replica bias), iref ≈ 5 µA, supplies iovdd / vdd 3.3 V (3.0 … 3.6 V). Documented linear input range 0 … ~1 V with vref = 0.5 V (high end 1.057 … 1.299 V over corners).
- D2S pad `sg13cmos5l_IOPadDiff2Single`: differential in (vinp, vinn around vcm), pad out: pad − vref = (vinp − vinn)/2, vref (of the D2S) = vcm ≈ 1.26 V. Output swing about 0.64 … 3.28 V at 3.3 V; INL is much worse at iovdd 3.0 V (up to 87 mV in the schematic CACE).
- Default loop in the slot: pad_d2s = vcm + (pad_s2d − vref).

Sources (read-only): taped-out slot repo /home/user/sg13cmos5l_cm_slot7 (branch `chipalooza2-documentation`; `pads/<cell>/<cell>.spice` and .gds; `verification/c2_gaps/d2s_pex/` has a working magic PEX netlist of the D2S pad and the method/run scripts incl. the ngspice option rshunt=1e12 needed for convergence; `verification/c2_gaps/slot_modes_bg/` has slot-level power-up methods). Design repository https://github.com/tatzelbrumm/sg13cmos5l_cm_ip__single2diff2single (public, ~160 MB: do NOT clone whole; `git clone --depth 1 --filter=blob:none --no-checkout <url> <dir>`, `git fetch --depth 1 origin <commit>`, `git show FETCH_HEAD:<path>`, `git archive FETCH_HEAD <paths> | tar x`): commit 91a7da8b67ac56a26c605501146667a1fde28c32 (pad macros incl. Reuse the existing CACE testbench conditions where possible so the graphs agree with the committed CACE tables. Scratch: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/468ace82-a4b3-592d-8838-6ea52a1be021/scratchpad/dcgraphs.
Environment: IHP PDK /foss/pdks/ihp-sg13cmos5l (PDK_ROOT=/foss/pdks PDK=ihp-sg13cmos5l), ngspice with IHP OSDI, magic, netgen, CACE 2.11, Python3 + matplotlib (install with pip --break-system-packages if missing). 4 CPUs; another job runs a light read-only task; use at most 3 parallel ngspice.

Graphs, in priority order (both schematic and post-layout PEX view of the taped-out pad where available; label which):
1. S2D DC transfer: outp, outn, differential output and output common mode vs pad input swept wide (e.g. −0.3 … 3.3 V, fine steps) at tt 27 °C 3.3 V, with the linear input range marked; then the differential output and the deviation from the ideal 2·(pad − vref) (INL-style error, mV, log or linear) over pad 0 … 1.3 V for corners tt/ss/ff/sf/fs × −40/27/125 °C (and 3.0/3.6 V supply at the worst corners). Define and state the linear-range criterion you use (e.g. error from the best-fit line < 1 mV or gain within 1 %), and give a table of the input range per corner by that criterion.

Table of output range per corner by your criterion.

# SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2026 Christoph Maier
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
"""Convert a Claude Code session transcript (JSONL) into a readable Markdown chat log.

Kept: user messages, assistant text, tool calls (name and the inputs that say what was done), tool results
(shortened), harness notifications (marked as such). Left out: the model's internal reasoning ("thinking"
blocks), injected ", re.S)
RESULT_MAX = 1500


def clean(text):
    text = SYSREM.sub("", text)
    text = MAIL.sub(lambda m: m.group(0) if m.group(0) in ALLOWED_MAIL else "[e-mail redacted]", text)
    text = re.sub(r"claude-(opus|sonnet|haiku|fable)-[0-9-]+", "[model id]", text)
    return text.strip()


def fence(text, lang=""):
    text = text.replace("ʼʼʼ", "ʼʼʼ")
    return f"ʼʼʼ{lang}\n{text}\nʼʼʼ"


def short(text, n=RESULT_MAX):
    return text if len(text) <= n else text[:n] + f"\n… [{len(text) - n} more characters cut]"


def tool_call(b):
    name, inp = b.get("name", "?"), b.get("input", {}) or {}
    out = [f"**Tool call: `{name}`**"]
    if "description" in inp:
        out.append(f"_{clean(str(inp['description']))}_")
    if name == "Bash":
        out.append(fence(clean(inp.get("command", "")), "bash"))
    elif name in ("Write",):
        out.append(f"file: `{inp.get('file_path')}`")
        out.append(fence(short(clean(inp.get("content", "")), 4000)))
    elif name in ("Edit",):
        out.append(f"file: `{inp.get('file_path')}`")
        out.append("old:\n" + fence(short(clean(inp.get("old_string", "")), 1500)))
        out.append("new:\n" + fence(short(clean(inp.get("new_string", "")), 1500)))
    elif name == "Agent":
        out.append(fence(clean(inp.get("prompt", ""))))
    else:
        rest = {k: v for k, v in inp.items() if k != "description"}
        if rest:
            out.append(fence(short(clean(json.dumps(rest, ensure_ascii=False, indent=1)), 3000), "json"))
    return "\n\n".join(out)


def tool_result(b):
    c = b.get("content")
    if isinstance(c, list):
        c = "\n".join(x.get("text", "[" + x.get("type", "?") + "]") for x in c if isinstance(x, dict))
    c = clean(str(c or ""))
    return "**Tool result:**\n\n" + fence(short(c)) if c else ""


def main(src, dst, title):
    lines = [f"# {title}", "", f"Generated by `chatlog/tools/jsonl_to_md.py` from the session transcript `{src.split('/')[-1]}`.",
             "Internal reasoning, injected system reminders and private e-mail addresses are left out; tool results are shortened.", ""]
    for raw in open(src, encoding="utf-8"):
        try:
            d = json.loads(raw)
        except ValueError:
            continue
        t = d.get("type")
        if t not in ("user", "assistant"):
            continue
        ts = d.get("timestamp", "")[:19].replace("T", " ")
        m = d.get("message") or {}
        c = m.get("content")
        blocks = [{"type": "text", "text": c}] if isinstance(c, str) else (c or [])
        parts = []
        for b in blocks:
            if not isinstance(b, dict):
                continue
            bt = b.get("type")
            if bt == "thinking" or bt == "redacted_thinking":
                continue
            if bt == "text":
                txt = clean(b.get("text", ""))
                if not txt:
                    continue
                if t == "user" and ("<task-notification>" in txt or txt.startswith("[SYSTEM NOTIFICATION")
                                    or "Monitor event" in txt):
                    parts.append("_Harness notification:_\n\n" + fence(short(txt, 800)))
                elif t == "user":
                    parts.append(txt)
                else:
                    parts.append(txt)
            elif bt == "image":
                parts.append("[image attached by the user]")
            elif bt == "tool_use":
                parts.append(tool_call(b))
            elif bt == "tool_result":
                r = tool_result(b)
                if r:
                    parts.append(r)
        if n

#!/bin/sh
# SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2026 Christoph Maier
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
# Regenerate the Markdown chat logs of the cloud session (main conversation + one per background agent).
# usage: export_all.sh <session transcript dir parent> <session id>
set -e
P=${1:-/root/.claude/projects/-home-user}; S=${2:-468ace82-a4b3-592d-8838-6ea52a1be021}
D=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")/.." && pwd); T="$D/tools/jsonl_to_md.py"
mkdir -p "$D/agents"
python3 "$T" "$P/$S.jsonl" "$D/session_2026-11-23_main.md" "Cloud session 2026-11-23/24: main conversation"
idx="$D/README.md"
{
echo "# Chat logs of the cloud session of 2026-11-23 / 24"
echo
echo "Generated from the session transcripts by \`tools/export_all.sh\` (converter \`tools/jsonl_to_md.py\`): user messages, assistant"
echo "replies, every tool call with its inputs, shortened tool results. Left out: the model's internal reasoning, injected system"
echo "reminders, private e-mail addresses. Times are UTC (CET = UTC+1). Regenerated at each push, so the newest part grows."
echo
echo "| log | what |"
echo "|---|---|"
echo "| [session_2026-11-23_main.md](session_2026-11-23_main.md) | the main conversation (user, coordinator) |"
for m in "$P/$S"/subagents/agent-*.meta.json; do
  id=$(basename "$m" .meta.json); j="$P/$S/subagents/$id.jsonl"; [ -f "$j" ] || continue
  desc=$(python3 -c "import json,sys;print(json.load(open(sys.argv[1])).get('description','agent'))" "$m")
  stopped=$(python3 -c "import json,sys;print('stopped by the user' if json.load(open(sys.argv[1])).get('stoppedByUser') else '')" "$m")
  start=$(python3 -c "import json,sys
for l in open(sys.argv[1]):
  d=json.loads(l)
  if d.get('timestamp'): print(d['timestamp'][:16].replace('T',' ')); break" "$j")
  slug=$(echo "$desc" | tr 'A-Z' 'a-z' | tr -c 'a-z0-9\n' '_' | sed 's/__*/_/g; s/_$//')
  out="agents/$(echo $start | cut -c1-10)_${slug}.md"
  python3 "$T" "$j" "$D/$out" "Background agent: $desc (started $start UTC)"
  echo "| [$out]($out) | background agent \"$desc\", started $start UTC ${stopped:+($stopped)} |"
done
} > "$idx"
EOF
chmod +x $SB/chatlog/tools/export_all.sh && $SB/chatlog/tools/export_all.sh && cat $SB/chatlog/README.md && du -sh $SB/chatlog; ls -la $SB/chatlog $SB/chatlog/agents | awk '{print $5, $9}'; grep -l -E '[A-Za-z0-9._%+-]+@gmail' -r $SB/chatlog | head; grep -c 'thinking' $SB/chatlog/session_2026-11-23_main.md

14:## 1. Cells
24:## 2. Inputs and fail-safe level shifting
42:## 3. Mode decode (derived from the netlist)
89:## 4. Switch matrix: ten transmission gates
103:## 5. Static ties and same-net joins in `slot_7`

116:## 7. What was verified, and what was not
133:## 8. Discrepancies to be aware of
## 4. Switch matrix: ten transmission gates

Each is a `tg_hv` (NMOS + PMOS, 1 µm / 0.45 µm hv, bulks on `vss` / `vddh`), closed when its control is 1 and its complement is 0:

| gate | control | connects | purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| XT1, XT2 | `ld` | S2D `outp`–D2S `vinp`, `outn`–`vinn` | loop direct |
| XT3, XT4 | `lx` | `outp`–`vinn`, `outn`–`vinp` | loop crossed |
| XT5, XT6 | `sb` | `outp`–`bus0`, `outn`–`bus1` | monitor / drive the S2D outputs |
| XT7, XT8 | `bd` | `bus0`–`vinp`, `bus1`–`vinn` | feed the D2S from outside |
| XT9, XT10 | `rc` | `vcm_rb`–`vcm_pad`, `vref_rb`–`vref_pad` | replica bias onto the pads |

Each control is an inverted NAND output (`ld` = inverter of `ld_b`, and so on); the complement pin of each gate is the NAND output `ld_b`, `lx_b`, `sb_b`, `bd_b`, `rc_b`. All gates are in the 3.3 V domain.

## 5. Static ties and same-net joins in `slot_7`

- **`bias_dist_pd` configuration:** input 0 (`ibg`, the bandgap's ~5 µA) on, inputs 1…3 tied to `vss_3v3` and their enables low; outputs 0…2 on (to `pad_ref_rb`'s `iprb`,
  `s7_an_0_iref`, `s7_an_1_iref`), output 3 off (`isp`, spare), its enable tied low. The three distrib

```
# SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2026 Christoph Maier
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
"""Draw the signal and current flow of slot 7 as taped out (158afc5) as an SVG block diagram.

Connections follow glue_logic.md (switch matrix XT1..XT10, same-net joins, bias_dist_pd configuration) and
README.md section 2. usage: python3 make_signal_flow.py [out.svg]   (default: slot7_signal_flow.svg next to this file)
"""
import os
import sys

W, H = 1240, 830
C_CTRL, C_IREF, C_VREF, C_SIG, C_BUS, C_TXT = "#7b3fa0", "#c25e00", "#2e7d32", "#1f5fbf", "#666666", "#222222"
out = []


def box(x, y, w, h, lines, fill="#f7f7f7", stroke="#444", bold_first=True, size=13):
    out.append(f'<rect x="{x}" y="{y}" width="{w}" height="{h}" rx="6" fill="{fill}" stroke="{stroke}" stroke-width="1.4"/>')
    ty = y + 20
    for i, t in enumerate(lines):
        wgt = "bold" if (i == 0 and bold_first) else "normal"
        out.append(f'<text x="{x + 10}" y="{ty}" font-size="{size + (1 if i == 0 and bold_first else 0)}" '
                   f'font-weight="{wgt}" fill="{C_TXT}">{t}</text>')
        ty += size + 5


def line(pts, color, width=2.0, dash=None, arrow=False):
    d = " ".join(f"{'M' if i == 0 else 'L'}{x},{y}" for i, (x, y) in enumerate(pts))
    extra = f' stroke-dasharray="{dash}"' if dash else ""
    mk = f' marker-end="url(#a_{color[1:]})"' if arrow else ""
    out.append(f'<path d="{d}" fill="none" stroke="{color}" stroke-width="{width}"{extra}{mk}/>')


def dot(x, y, color):
    out.append(f'<circle cx="{x}" cy="{y}" r="3.6" fill="{color}"/>')


def text(x, y, t, color=C_TXT, size=12, anchor="start", weight="normal", style="normal"):
    out.append(f'<text x="{x}" y="{y}" font-size="{size}" fill="{color}" text-anchor="{anchor}" '
               f'font-weight="{weight}" font-style="{style}">{t}</text>')


def switch(x, y, color, label=None):
    """Open switch symbol between (x, y) and (x + 36, y)."""
    line([(x, y), (x + 10, y)], color)
    line([(x + 10, y), (x + 27, y - 9)], color)
    line([(x + 27, y), (x + 36, y)], color)
    dot(x + 10, y, color)
    dot(x + 27, y, color)
    if label:
        text(x + 18, y - 13, label, C_CTRL, 11, "middle")


def bondpad(x, y, label):
    out.append(f'<rect x="{x}" y="{y}" width="50" height="50" fill="#e8d9a8" stroke="#8a6d1a" stroke-width="1.5"/>')
    out.append(f'<rect x="{x + 8}" y="{y + 8}" width="34" height="34" fill="none" stroke="#8a6d1a" stroke-width="1"/>')
    text(x + 25, y + 68, label, C_TXT, 12, "middle", "bold")


# ---- top row: harness controls, s7_ctrl, legend
box(20, 45, 185, 80, ["harness", "enable, dig_in[0..5]", "1.2 V levels, always-on rail"])
line([(205, 85), (248, 85)], C_CTRL, arrow=True)
box(250, 25, 420, 112, ["s7_ctrl: 3.3 V glue logic (71.6 × 38.04 µm)",
                        "7 fail-safe level shifters ls_up_fs (1.2 V → 3.3 V)",
                        "key: dig_in[3] = 1 and dig_in[4] = 0, else default",
                        "mode decode → switch controls ld, lx, sb, bd, rc",
                        "en / en_b to every block (enable = 0: all off)"], fill="#f3ecf7", stroke=C_CTRL)
# legend
lx, ly = 905, 25
out.append(f'<rect x="{lx}" y="{ly}" width="315" height="135" rx="6" fill="#ffffff" stroke="#999"/>')
text(lx + 10, ly + 19, "legend", C_TXT, 13, weight="bold")
for i, (c, dash, w, t) in enumerate([(C_CTRL, "6,4", 2, "control (3.3 V enables, switch controls)"),
                                      (C_IREF, None, 2.4, "reference current (≈ 5 µA, sourced)"),
                                      (C_VREF, None, 2.4, "reference voltage (vcm, vref)"),
                                      (C_SIG, None, 2.4, "signal path"),
                                      (C_BUS, None, 4, "harness shared analog bus")]):
    yy = ly + 40 + 20 * i
    line([(lx + 12, yy - 4), (lx + 52, yy - 4)], c, w, dash)
    text(lx + 62, yy, t, C_TXT, 12)

# ---- bias row
box(20, 190, 190, 82, ["bg_core_pd", "Banba current-mode bandgap", "sources ≈ 5 µA (no op amp)"], fill="#fff4e8", stroke=C_IR
… [5829 more characters cut]
```

## Assistant — 2026-11-25 00:11:57 UTC

The DC graphs are pushed to `chipalooza2-documentation` (`c4b33f5`), in `verification/c2_gaps/dc_curves/`:
https://github.com/tatzelbrumm/sg13cmos5l_cm_slot7/tree/chipalooza2-documentation/verification/c2_gaps/dc_curves

There are 36 plots in `plots/` (each as PNG and SVG), plus `README.md` (what each figure shows and how to read it), `results.md` (range tables and key numbers), the tables per corner in `tables/` and `run.sh`. The restart lost nothing: a rerun of `run.sh` in a fresh folder reproduced every table byte for byte. The pads' own CACE testbench was used, and the numbers agree with the committed CACE results to within 0.6 mV.

**Figures to start with:**

| figure | shows |
|---|---|
| `s2d_transfer_tt27_pex.png` (also `_schematic`) | S2D `outp`, `outn`, difference and common mode over pad input −0.3 … 3.3 V; linear range shaded |
| `s2d_corners_*.png` | S2D difference and error from the ideal gain of 2, over 5 corners × 3 temperatures |
| `s2d_range.png`, `s2d_range_slot.png` | S2D input range per corner and supply, at the test setting and at the slot's `vref` / `vcm` |
| `s2d_vrefvcm_range.png` | S2D input range against the `vref` and `vcm` settings |
| `d2s_transfer_tt27_*.png`, `d2s_corners_*.png` | D2S pad output and its error over differential input, loaded and unloaded, at 3.0 V and 3.3 V |
| `d2s_range*.png` | D2S output range per corner and supply |
| `rt_transfer_tt27.png`, `rt_error_corners.png` | round trip S2D → D2S in the slot, and its error across corners |

The `*_linearity_criterion.png` figures show how the range ends are defined:
- **S2D:** error under 1 mV, input-referred, as in the CACE file.
- **D2S:** error under 10 mV, output-referred, the CACE INL limit.

**Results** (IHP sg13cmos5l simulation, schematic and extracted layout):
- **S2D input range** (`vref` 0.5 V): the high end is 1.057 V at worst (ss, −40 °C, 3.0 V) and up to 1.299 V. At tt 27 °C, 3.3 V the range is −0.149 … 1.245 V. At the slot's `vcm` of 1.26 V the high end drops to 0.980 V at ss / −40 °C / 3.0 V.
- **D2S output range** at 3.3 V: 1.121 … 2.180 V at the worst corner, 0.985 … 2.314 V at tt 27 °C. The pad output clips at 0.64 … 0.74 V and 3.27 … 3.28 V.
- **The large D2S INL at 3.0 V (87 mV) is an artefact of the test condition.** It comes from CACE's input common mode of 1.65 V. At the slot's actual 1.26 V, INL stays at or below 1.38 mV even at 3.0 V. Row 16 of the proposal currently lists the 3.0 V case as TBD with a "headroom limit"; this result answers it.
- **Round trip:** 0.86 mV at most over 0 … 1 V at tt 27 °C, 6.1 mV at ff 125 °C 3.6 V.
- **Layout effect:** extracted layout and schematic agree within 0.7 mV on range ends and 0.03 mV on the error curves.

**Two things in the figures you should look at:**
- **S2D:** at about 1.53 V input both outputs jump abruptly to their limits instead of clipping gradually (`s2d_transfer_tt27_pex.png`).
- **D2S:** past about +1.75 V differential input the output snaps to the upper rail (`d2s_corners_pex.png`). On the negative side it clips smoothly.

Both are well outside the linear ranges. I haven't investigated either.

**Open points the job reported:**
- The D2S needs the simulator's 1 TΩ resistor from every node to ground in both views; it makes no difference to the results.
- The rerun D2S INL at 3.0 V is 1.2 % above the committed CACE value, and the job did not find the cause.
- One round-trip run on the extracted layout (sf, −40 °C) stopped at 1.435 V input, after the linear range.
- The KLayout-extracted pads move the round-trip range ends by up to 55 mV. Not investigated.

The graphs are not yet embedded in the slot 7 description or the data sheets. Tell me which ones you want there, or which to change, and I'll make more. Separately, shall I correct row 16 of the proposal with the 1.26 V result?

cd /home/user/sg13cmos5l_cm_slot7/docs/chipalooza2_as_taped_out && python3 - <<'EOF'
p='README.md'; s=open(p).read()
title="# Slot 7: custom S2D / D2S pad pair with a bandgap-referenced replica bias (as taped out, November 2026)\n"
i0=s.index(title)+len(title)
i1=s.index("## 1. Which version was taped out")
old=s[i0:i1]
bq_start=old.index("> **Where this set comes from.**")

D="../../verification/c2_gaps/"
top=f'''
Designer: Christoph Maier. Chipalooza Challenge #2 (IHP sg13cmos5l), slot 7, taped out on 2026-11-09 as commit `158afc5` of
this repository. No silicon results exist yet: every number below is a **simulation** (IHP sg13cmos5l; schematic or extracted
layout as stated), and the design had no specification before it was characterized (proof of concept).

## Slot 7 at a glance

**What it is.** Two custom analog I/O pads that turn a single-ended pad voltage into a differential pair inside the chip
(**S2D**, input pad `s7_an[0]`) and a differential pair back into a pad voltage (**D2S**, output pad `s7_an[1]`), with the gain
set by matched devices (2 and ½), not by resistors. A small on-slot bandgap, a bias distribution and a replica-bias block give
both pads their 5 µA references and their common-mode / reference voltages; 3.3 V glue with fail-safe enables selects the modes.
In the default mode the D2S pad reproduces the S2D pad voltage, shifted by `vcm − vref` ≈ 0.77 V.

![Signal and current flow of slot 7](pix/slot7_signal_flow.svg)

*Figure 1: blocks, references and signal path (details in [§2.1](#21-signal-and-current-flow) and [glue_logic.md](glue_logic.md)).*

![Round trip S2D → D2S, DC transfer at tt 27 °C](../../verification/c2_gaps/dc_curves/plots/rt_transfer_tt27.png)

*Figure 2: what the slot does, end to end: D2S pad output vs S2D pad input in the default mode, tt 27 °C 3.3 V, with the
slot's own replica `vcm` / `vref`. The shaded band is the input range where the output is within 1 mV of the straight line:
−0.171 … 1.281 V in → 0.595 … 2.044 V out. Schematic, extracted (magic PEX) and KLayout-extracted pads lie on top of each
other. Above about 1.5 V input the S2D outputs saturate and the output stops at about 2.25 V.*

![Round trip error over corners](../../verification/c2_gaps/dc_curves/plots/rt_error_corners.png)

*Figure 3: the same as an error (pad out − expected), at the corner extremes of the slot simulation. Over the intended input
range 0 … 1 V the error is at most 0.86 mV at tt 27 °C and 6.1 mV at ff 125 °C 3.6 V.*

![S2D pad DC transfer](../../verification/c2_gaps/dc_curves/plots/s2d_transfer_tt27_pex.png)

*Figure 4: the input pad alone (S2D, extracted layout): `outp`, `outn`, their difference (= 2 (pad − vref)) and their common
mode vs pad voltage; shaded: linear input range (error < 1 mV input-referred).*

![D2S pad over corners at the slot operating point](../../verification/c2_gaps/dc_curves/plots/d2s_corners_slot_schematic.png)

*Figure 5: the output pad alone (D2S) at the slot's operating point (input common mode = vref = 1.26 V, 1 kΩ + 100 pF load),
5 process corners × 3 temperatures: output (top) and error from the ideal ½ (vinp − vinn) (bottom, shaded ±10 mV).*

### Key numbers (simulation)

| quantity | value | conditions | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| S2D gain (outp − outn)/(pad − vref) | 1.9985 … 1.9993 | 5 corners × 3 T × 3.0 … 3.6 V; schematic = extracted | [S2D data sheet](datasheets/sg13cmos5l_IOPadSingle2Diff.md) |
| S2D linear input range (1 mV) | −0.157 … 1.296 V at tt 27 °C 3.3 V; high end ≥ 0.980 V at every corner | vref 0.49 V, vcm 1.26 V | [DC curves](../../verification/c2_gaps/dc_curves/results.md) §1b |
| S2D −3 dB bandwidth | 1.24 … 1.67 MHz | extracted layout, 5 corners × 3 T | [S2D data sheet](datasheets/sg13cmos5l_IOPadSingle2Diff.md) |
| D2S gain pad / (vinp − vinn) | 0.4983 … 0.5000 | schematic and extracted | [DC curves](../../verification/c2_gaps/dc_curves/results.md) §2b |
| D2S INL over ±1 V differential | ≤ 0.56 mV at 3.3 V, ≤ 1.38 mV at 3.0 V | slot operating point (vicm 1.26 V), 1 kΩ | [DC curves](../../verification/c2_gaps/dc_curves/results.md) §2b |
| D2S linear output range (10 mV) | 0.211 … 2.317 V at tt 27 °C; worst 0.328 … 2.258 V | 3.3 V, slot operating point, 1 kΩ | [DC curves](../../verification/c2_gaps/dc_curves/results.md) §2b |
| D2S bandwidth, slew rate | 1.43 … 2.71 MHz, 1.59 … 2.79 V/µs | extracted layout, 10 … 300 pF | [D2S post-layout](../../verification/c2_gaps/d2s_pex/results.md) |
| round-trip error, pad in 0 … 1 V | ≤ 0.86 mV (tt 27 °C); ≤ 6.5 mV (ff 125 °C 3.6 V) | default mode, replica bias | [slot data sheet](datasheets/slot_7_system.md), Figure 3 |
| `vcm` / `vref` from the replica | 1.115 … 1.395 V / 0.436 … 0.596 V; mismatch σ 5.4 / 3.8 mV | corners × −40 … 125 °C × 3.0 … 3.6 V | [replica data sheet](datasheets/pad_ref_rb.md), [replica bias](../../verification/c2_gaps/replica_bias/results.md) |
| bandgap reference current | 3.83 … 6.37 µA (typ. 4.96 µA) | set by the resistor corner (−21 % / +28 %) | [bandgap](../../verification/c2_gaps/slot_modes_bg/results.md) |
| supply current, enabled | slot 48.4 … 49.7 µA; pad ring 365 … 396 µA | 5 corners | [slot data sheet](datasheets/slot_7_system.md) |
| supply current, disabled / pad leakage unpowered | slot 25 … 56 nA, ring 9 … 58 nA / ≤ 67 nA at 125 °C | | [slot data sheet](datasheets/slot_7_system.md), §2.2 |

**Verification status.** Slot layout: KLayout DRC clean, LVS match (497 devices); both pads DRC clean and KLayout LVS match
(magic LVS: only the accepted `vss` / `iovss` substrate merge); the chip GDS contains `158afc5` unchanged (XOR 0). Not done:
PEX of the whole slot, ESD / latch-up simulation of the pads, silicon measurement. The Oguey–Aebischer core `oa_core_pd` is
**not** in the taped-out slot (see [§8](#8-about-this-document-set)). Details: [§5](#5-verification-state), [signoff](../../signoff/README.md).

## Where to find what

| question | go to |
|---|---|
| How is the slot built, block by block? | [§2](#2-electrical-function); schematics: [pix/schematics/](pix/schematics/README.md); layout: [pix/layout/](pix/layout/README.md) |
| What do the modes and the `dig_in` bits do? | [§2.4](#24-modes), [glue_logic.md](glue_logic.md) |
| Which pins, where? | [§4](#4-physical-interface-and-layout-hierarchy) |
| What does each pad do, in numbers? | data sheets: [S2D](datasheets/sg13cmos5l_IOPadSingle2Diff.md), [D2S](datasheets/sg13cmos5l_IOPadDiff2Single.md), [replica bias](datasheets/pad_ref_rb.md), [whole slot](datasheets/slot_7_system.md) ([how the data sheets were made](datasheets/README.md)) |
| DC transfer, input / output range, linearity | [DC curves](../../verification/c2_gaps/dc_curves/README.md) (36 figures; read `results.md` first) |
| What was verified, and what is open? | [§3](#3-readiness-of-the-blocks-that-were-kicked-off), [§5](#5-verification-state), [§5a](#5a-gap-simulations-of-2026-11-23), [signoff](../../signoff/README.md) |
| How to measure the chip | [TEST_PLAN.md](TEST_PLAN.md) |
| Earlier test platforms (not taped out) | [test_platforms.md](test_platforms.md) |
| How this set was made; AI assistance | [§8](#8-about-this-document-set), [log.md](log.md), [../AI_ASSISTANCE.md](../AI_ASSISTANCE.md) |

'''
s=s[:i0]+top+s[i1:]
s=s.rstrip()+"\n\n## 8. About this document set\n\n"+blockquote+"\n\n"+intro.replace("Designer: Christoph Maier. ","Original introduction: ")+"\n"
open(p,'w').write(s)
EOF
grep -n '^#' README.md
```

## User (or harness) — 2026-11-25 02:36:29 UTC

could you speed up the tasks by spawning agents, or is it not worth the overhead?

## Assistant — 2026-11-25 02:36:40 UTC

It isn't worth splitting the simulation work. The bottleneck is the machine, not the number of agents:

- **CPU-bound:** this container has 4 CPUs, and the AC / transient job already runs 3 simulations in parallel. More simulation agents would only compete for the same CPUs. Each would also spend its first minutes re-reading the earlier work and fetching the design repository, and the runs would take longer, not shorter.
- **One job is easier to keep consistent:** with a single job, all AC / transient figures share one style, one set of operating points and one cross-check against CACE. Split across agents, that is where errors creep in, and I'd spend the saved time reconciling them.
- **Token cost:** every agent you spawn costs tokens for its own startup and reading, which you pay for.

Where a second agent would help is work that needs almost no CPU. It can run alongside the simulations:

1. **Design rationale.** Write "how it works" sections for both pads and the replica bias: why the matched devices give exactly 2 and ½, how `vcm` and `vref` are placed, why the D2S output stage sits in the pad's clamp structure, the Banba relation. The gap analysis found ours to be the only project without this. It would be written from the schematics and the design repository's READMEs, with nothing invented.
2. **Plots from data already committed.** For example: bandgap current against temperature per corner, extracted layout against schematic as a bar chart per pad, and the mismatch histogram of the bias distribution. These are cheap matplotlib work.

Sign-off of the taped-out GDS with the art (DRC and LVS) is also missing, but it is CPU-heavy. I'd run it after the simulations rather than alongside.

My suggestion: one extra agent now for items 1 and 2, and leave the simulations as they are. Shall I start it?

The pads' committed CACE testbenches run unchanged: lines are only added inside `.control`, so each run's CACE scalars come out exactly as before. ngspice-47 and CACE 2.11.0.
  
  **Figures** (schematic and magic PEX unless noted):
  - Round trip: `rt_bode_tt27` (3 loads × schematic / PEX / KLayout pads), `rt_bode_pvt`, `rt_f3db`, `rt_step_tt27` (settling marked), `rt_step_pvt`, `rt_sine_tt27` (output − expected, distortion ×10, THD).
  - S2D: `s2d_bode`, `s2d_cm_response`, `s2d_step` (0.001 / 0.5 pF), `s2d_settling`, `s2d_noise`, `s2d_enable` (enable and disable).
  - D2S: `d2s_bode_loads` (10 / 100 / 300 pF / 1 nF, 1 kΩ or unloaded), `d2s_bode_corners`, `d2s_zout`, `d2s_psrr`, `d2s_step`, `d2s_slot_vs_cace` (vicm 1.26 V vs 1.65 V), `d2s_noise`, `d2s_enable`.
  
  **Key numbers:**
  - **Round trip, tt 27 °C, bandwidth (schematic / PEX / KLayout):** 1.53 / 1.36 / 1.51 MHz unloaded; 1.27 / 1.12 / 1.22 MHz with 1 kΩ ∥ 10 pF.
    - Over the 5 PVT points: 1.08–1.69 MHz, no peaking in any run.
    - 1 % settling: 453–557 ns schematic, 517–623 ns PEX.
    - THD at 10 kHz, 0–1 V: −78 dB at tt; −53 dB at ff 125 °C, where 0 V is below the S2D's linear input range.
  - **S2D bandwidth:** 1.35–1.81 MHz schematic, 1.24–1.66 MHz PEX. Temperature sets it; the corner barely matters.
  - **S2D noise:** 680 nV/√Hz at 1 kHz and 153 nV/√Hz at 100 kHz.
  - **D2S peaking:** 4.8 dB at 1 kΩ + 1 nF, 7.4 dB at 1 nF unloaded.
  - **D2S PSRR:** 70.6–72.5 dB at 1 kHz.
  - **D2S slew (tt, 10 / 100 / 300 pF):** 1.84 / 2.43 / 2.92 V/µs.
  
  **Cross-check** (244 comparisons):
  - **S2D, both views, and D2S PEX:** this run's CACE values are identical to the committed CSVs. Values read from the curves are within 0.1 %.
  - **D2S schematic:** 24 values differ by more than 2 % (Zout at 1 kHz down to −14 %, t_on up to +53 %), and CACE's own output differs just as much. Cause: those committed CSVs date from 2026-10-21, while the D2S schematic changed until 2026-11-03 (design-repo history). They are marked "stale ref". Same templates reproduce the S2D and D2S PEX results exactly, and the rshunt solver option changes the D2S schematic results by at most 0.006 %.
  - **Round trip:** there are no committed CACE values. The gain at 10 Hz and the step gain agree with the `../dc_curves` DC slope within 0.02 %, and the replica operating point matches to 4 digits.
  
  **Failures / TBDs:**
  - **KLayout sine:** the 10 kHz sine through the KLayout-extracted pads aborts with "timestep too small" at pad ≈ 0.078 V, whatever solver aid I tried. Cause not found.
  - **Cold schematic slot runs:** ss −40 °C and sf −40 °C needed an automatic retry with rshunt to find the operating point. These runs are flagged; forcing rshunt on the tt run changes the bandwidth by only 0.0005 %.
  - **CACE unit labels:** S2D `Vcm_dev` and D2S `Glitch` are labelled mV but computed in V. So Vcm_dev's 50 "mV" limit is effectively 50 V.
  - **Not covered:** mismatch (so no S2D PSRR/CMRR) and round-trip noise. PEX round-trip steps were run at three PVT points only.
  
  Start with `README.md` and `results.md`; `./run.sh <work_dir> plots` regenerates everything from the work folder.
